HYPERION belgesindeki devrim iddiaları (2.5 ns yaşam döngüsü, sıfır gecikmeli ORAM, kıtalararası tek adres uzayı) docs/HYPERION_ASSESSMENT.md §1'de teorem/fizik ile çürütüldü. CMF bunları taklit etmez. Devrim, ölçülebilir tek yerde duruyor: Bir program artık "derlenmiş ya da derlenmemiş" değil; her witness için ayrı ayrı derlenmiş ve reddedilenlerin var olmadığı bir varyant kümesidir.* Bunu iddia etmek için gereken tek şey, bunu yapan bir komut ve onu sabitleyen testlerdir; ikisi de bu repoda var. ZigLIR is the Zig-written compiler for the LIR language; its installed CLI is lir. The lexer, parser, semantic analyzer, IR, optimizer, x86-64 ELF emitter, and wasm32-wasi emitter are implemented in Zig. The direct ELF/WASM build path does not invoke LLVM, GCC, or Clang. The portable IR VM is also implemented in Zig. An optional C11 backend, itself implemented in Zig, emits C source for users who want to compile to additional targets with an external C compiler. The design optimizes in this order: Preserve language semantics and reject invalid programs. Make compiler invariants explicit and checkable. Produce deterministic, inspectable output. Optimize only transformations with correctness evidence. Measure compile time, runtime, memory, and binary size independently. No engineering process can prove a general compiler has zero defect probability. The goal is to make incorrect output difficult to introduce, easy to detect, and impossible to silently treat as a successful build. Target pipeline Source bytes -> Lexer (source spans + structured diagnostics) -> Parser (bounded AST + recovery) -> Name resolution -> Type checking and semantic validation -> Typed, canonical IR -> IR verifier -> Optimization pipeline (each pass preserves verified invariants) -> Lowered machine IR (explicit control flow, ABI, and stack effects) -> Register allocation / spilling -> x86-64 instruction selection and encoding -> Relocation and range validation -> ELF layout validation -> Atomic output write The existing implementation has only part of this pipeline. Do not label later stages complete until they exist and have tests. In particular, parser acceptance is not semantic validation, and producing bytes is not proof those bytes implement the source program. Stage contracts Source and diagnostics Every token stores a source span and starting line/column. Invalid characters, malformed strings, malformed numeric separators, and integer overflow are hard errors. Diagnostics are data returned by compiler APIs; parsing and code generation must not call process-exit routines. Recovery must always advance the cursor or terminate at EOF. Add a progress invariant to parser loops. Capacity exhaustion is a structured error, never a partially valid AST or executable. Parsing and semantic analysis Parsing constructs syntax only; it must not silently decide types or pretend unsupported syntax is valid. Resolve every identifier and function reference before machine-code generation. Reject duplicate declarations, unknown names, invalid argument counts, invalid return paths, invalid control-flow targets, and unsupported constructs explicitly. Type checking must precede optimizations that rely on types. Until ownership and borrowing checks exist, do not promise memory safety or ownership safety. AST and symbol-table capacity limits must be explicit, checked before writes, and tested at limit-1, limit, and limit+1. Canonical IR Use a documented, typed IR with explicit basic blocks and terminators. Every block must end in exactly one terminator; every branch target must exist; each value must have one definition if using SSA; every use must be dominated by its definition; function calls must obey a defined calling convention. Before and after each optimization pass, run the IR verifier in debug/test builds. Release builds may disable expensive duplicate checks only after inputs have been validated and verifier coverage is strong. An invalid IR is a compiler bug and must stop code generation. Do not add speculative optimizations to the current ad-hoc IR. First define semantics for integer overflow, division edge cases, shifts, booleans, pointer arithmetic, and undefined behavior. Optimizations must preserve those semantics exactly. Optimization strategy Implement in small, independently testable steps: Constant folding with checked arithmetic and explicit overflow semantics. Local simplification and dead-code elimination. Control-flow simplification and unreachable-block removal. SSA construction, dominance, and verified sparse conditional constant propagation. Liveness analysis and register allocation. Target-aware instruction selection and peephole rules. Optional inlining, loop transforms, and vectorization only after profiling shows a real bottleneck. Every optimization rule needs: A written precondition and semantic justification. Positive and negative unit tests. Differential tests against an unoptimized build. Randomized or generated programs where feasible. A benchmark showing repeatable benefit. A simple way to disable the pass when diagnosing miscompilations. Never optimize by assuming signed overflow cannot occur unless the language specification explicitly grants that assumption. Never fold expressions with side effects, traps, or unspecified semantics as if they were pure. Machine-code backend Define the System V AMD64 ABI contract centrally: argument registers, return registers, caller/callee-saved registers, stack alignment, red-zone policy, and call-clobber set. Use a single instruction encoder with checked buffer writes and typed register/operand representations; avoid scattered raw byte emission where possible. Track stack depth and alignment through every control-flow edge. At a merge, incoming stack states must agree. Register allocation must use liveness information and correct spill slots. A virtual register must never alias another live value's storage. Validate all branch displacements and relocation ranges before patching. Out-of-range branches must be expanded or rejected, never truncated. Verify function entry/exit paths restore the stack and callee-saved registers. Generate only supported instructions and reject unsupported IR operations rather than emitting placeholders. ELF output and file safety Validate all section/program-header offsets, sizes, alignments, entry points, and output-buffer lengths before writing. Use checked arithmetic for every size and offset computation. Do not return a successful artifact after any encoder, relocation, or layout error. Write to a temporary file, check every write and close result, then atomically rename to the destination. A failed compilation must not leave a new partial executable. Set executable permissions only after a complete output is successfully written. Test generated ELF headers and loadable segments independently of running the output. Performance architecture Prioritize structural wins over fragile micro-optimizations: Keep tokenization allocation-free where it remains simple and correct. Use arenas for per-compilation AST, IR, and temporary metadata; release the entire arena at compilation end. Avoid allocating large fixed arrays on the host stack. Allocate large compiler state from a per-compilation arena or heap with explicit error handling. Use compact integer IDs and contiguous arrays for tokens, blocks, instructions, and symbols. Avoid repeated name scans: build symbol tables and resolve names once. Avoid repeated whole-module walks when an incremental worklist suffices. Use a linear-scan allocator first if it is simpler and correct; evaluate graph coloring only when benchmarks justify its complexity. Add parallel compilation only at module boundaries with deterministic diagnostics and no shared mutable compiler state. Treat cache behavior, instruction count, and compile-time cost as measurable hypotheses, not assumptions. Do not claim that stack spilling, register allocation, SSA, or a fast path is complete merely because a data structure exists. Completion requires tests and runtime evidence. Correctness gates A change is not complete until relevant gates pass: Unit tests: lexer, parser, semantic analysis, IR, encoder, relocation, and ELF layout. Boundary tests: every fixed-capacity structure at its boundary and just beyond it. Negative tests: malformed source must fail nonzero and must not produce a successful output file. Differential tests: optimized and unoptimized outputs agree on program results and observable behavior. Runtime tests: generated executables run with expected exit codes, stdout, and stderr. ABI tests: nested calls, more arguments than register arguments, recursion, stack alignment, and callee-saved register preservation. Fuzzing: arbitrary bytes for lexer/parser and generated valid programs for compiler passes; crashes, hangs, and silent miscompiles are failures. Reproducibility: same source, compiler revision, and target produce deterministic output unless nondeterminism is explicitly documented. CI matrix: Debug and ReleaseFast; tests must run on every push and pull request. Performance gate: a performance change must not regress correctness, compile-time memory, or unrelated benchmark workloads without an explicit trade-off. For every discovered miscompile, preserve the source as a regression fixture. Signed division by invariant positive constants The native x86-64 backend lowers signed division and remainder by a known, positive, non-power-of-two constant using a 64-bit magic multiplier and shift rather than idiv. It applies the signed high-half correction and adjusts the arithmetic shift result toward zero, matching LIR's truncating signed division; remainder is computed as dividend - quotient * divisor. The existing power-of- two path remains separate. Dynamic and negative divisors keep the guarded hardware-division path.
<veriayrimi>
This lowering is target-specific, not an IR semantic change. Regression tests compare exact results against Python integer arithmetic across the full signed input boundaries, small and large positive divisors, negative divisors, and all four backends/optimization levels. Keep a lowering only with these correctness gates and repeatable benchmark evidence. Performance measurement Compare against a recent optimizing C compiler using equivalent algorithms, input sizes, integer overflow behavior, and observable outputs. Report CPU, OS, compiler versions, flags, warm-up policy, number of repetitions, median, dispersion, binary size, compile time, and peak RSS. Prevent dead-code elimination and verify every result. Do not use one microbenchmark to claim general superiority over C. Track at minimum: Compiler wall time and source throughput. Peak resident memory and allocation count. Generated code size. Runtime throughput and startup latency. Number of spills, loads/stores, branches, and dynamic instructions where measurement tools permit. Keep a baseline artifact and compare on stable runners. Use broad regression thresholds and repeat suspicious results before failing CI. Implementation order Gate A — Stabilize the existing compiler Make every compiler stage return structured errors; remove process termination from reusable compiler modules. Add progress guards and capacity checks to every fixed-size table and every write. Make output creation atomic and ensure failures cannot leave a success-looking executable. Add boundary and negative tests; run the full CI suite. Gate B — Establish semantics Document the implemented LIR subset and integer semantics. Add name resolution, duplicate-name checks, argument validation, and type checking. Reject unsupported syntax before IR lowering. Gate C — Make IR verifiable Specify the IR and control-flow invariants. Add a verifier and run it around every optimization pass. Add differential tests with optimizations enabled and disabled. Gate D — Improve generated code Centralize ABI and instruction encoding. Add correct liveness analysis, register allocation, and spill handling. Validate stack alignment, call clobbers, branch ranges, and relocations. Add disassembly and execution-based regression tests. Gate E — Optimize by evidence Establish C comparison benchmarks and LIR baselines. Add one optimization at a time, retaining only correctness-proven improvements. Profile real programs before attempting inlining, vectorization, or parallel compilation. Definition of done A feature is complete only when its semantics are documented, errors are explicit, tests cover positive/negative/boundary cases, CI passes, and its implementation is integrated end-to-end. A compiler is not production-ready merely because it builds, emits ELF, or passes a few example programs. String interning performance The IR module and native code generator intern strings with fixed-capacity open-addressed hash tables (512 buckets for at most 256 entries). FNV-1a hashes narrow candidate matches, while length and byte equality remain the final correctness check. This changes repeated string lookup from a linear scan of all prior literals to expected constant-time lookup without heap allocation; capacity and byte-buffer bounds remain explicitly checked. Thanks for helping. This project has one unusual rule that everything else follows from: The compiler is the documentation. If a feature is documented it must exist, be tested, and be listed in src/bootstrap/langref.zig. If it does not exist, it must be listed as unsupported with an avoid note. Repository map src/bootstrap/ the compiler (Zig 0.13) lexer.zig tokens, keyword table, literals parser.zig AST + recursive-descent parser (ASTNode has 3 children + flags) expand.zig generics/comptime/test expansion, Option/Result synthesis sema.zig name resolution, type checking, borrow/UAF/purity/contract analysis optimizer.zig AST-level constant folding and simplification ir.zig SSA IR data structures ir_gen.zig AST → IR lowering ir_opt.zig mem2reg, GVN, LICM, unrolling, magic division, DCE ir_verify.zig structural IR verifier (runs before and after optimization) ir_codegen.zig x86-64 ELF64 emitter wasm_backend.zig wasm32-wasi emitter c_backend.zig portable C11 emitter / cross-target driver ir_vm.zig portable in-process VM target.zig triples, backends, ABI descriptors driver.zig compile pipeline orchestration diag.zig coded diagnostics + human/short/JSON rendering langref.zig language + builtin catalogue ← single source of truth langcmd.zig lir lang, lir builtins explain.zig lir explain knowledge base (topics + LIR-Exxxx codes) main.zig CLI project.zig lir init scaffolding tests/ Python regression suites (CLI, backends, core, AI contracts) examples/ runnable programs; canonical-tour.lir is the reference docs/ SPEC, AI contract, generated reference, roadmap Build and test zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseFast zig build test # unit + catalogue conformance python3 tests/cli_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir python3 tests/ai_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir # machine contracts python3 tests/core_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir python3 tests/call_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir python3 tests/backend_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir # needs cc + node 22+ python3 tests/project_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir python3 tests/browser_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir The order matters; each step is a place where drift is caught. Implement it in the frontend/IR/backends, with the same diagnostics style as the surrounding code (failAt/reportCoded + a stable diag.Code). Add a Feature entry to src/bootstrap/langref.zig: status = .supported only if all backends agree; example must be a complete program (fn main), because the test suite compiles it; notes must state limits and traps; avoid must name the near-miss spellings that do not compile. Add negative coverage: put the rejected spellings into tests/ai_test.py::test_unsupported_spellings_are_rejected (or a core test). Regenerate the reference: ./zig-out/bin/lir lang --markdown > docs/LANGUAGE_REFERENCE.md. Update docs/SPEC.md if the feature changes the language contract, and docs/ROADMAP.md if it closes a planned item. Run the full gate list in §2. If you cannot finish a feature, land it as status = .partial (with the limit in notes) or .unsupported (with avoid). Half-documented work is worse than none. Adding a builtin Register it in sema.zig (builtins table) — that is what type-checking consults. Lower it in ir_gen.zig (builtin_op table) and emit it in every backend it claims to support (ir_codegen.zig, wasm_backend.zig, c_backend.zig, ir_vm.zig). A backend that cannot express it must reject it at compile time with LIR-E7004, never trap at runtime. Document it in langref.builtins with a signature and trap notes. Add a runtime test that exercises it on --backend=vm, native and c. Documentation workflow Wording rules: say what the bootstrap compiler does today; never describe a roadmap item as implemented; prefer “rejected with LIR-Exxxx” over “not yet supported” when the compiler actually fails. Performance changes Follow docs/ROADMAP.md §“Required optimization workflow”: show a baseline, use identical workloads and flags, verify outputs for correctness, report median and spread, and inspect generated code. Keep an optimization only if the benchmark gate in bench/ shows a repeatable win at -O2. Diagnostics style One diagnostic per problem, with the narrowest span that identifies it. Message form: what is wrong, then what was expected: expected i64, found string. Use expectAt so JSON consumers get expect{kind,value}. Add a helpAt when there is a concrete fix. Never silently accept unsupported syntax; fail closed with a code. Pull requests One logical change per PR; describe which gate commands you ran. Do not reformat unrelated files. If you change a diagnostic message, keep the code stable — tooling keys on the code, not the text. Keep generated artifacts and large data out of Git. This is an incremental implementation, not parity with C++, Java, Rust, Go, Elixir, Odin, or Python. Complete generic argument/lifetime inference, ownership checking, and recursive ADT exhaustiveness remain separate projects. New patterns Tuple patterns use the existing tuple syntax and support nesting, literals, wildcards, and immutable bindings: fn describe(value: ((i64, bool), string)) -> i64 { return match value { ((1, true), "ready") => 100, ((number, _), text) if text == "ready" => number, ((_, _), _) => -1, } } The scrutinee is evaluated once. Fields use the existing eight-byte-slot tuple layout. Bindings are available to that arm's guard and body only, retain their field types, and are not available after the match. Duplicate binding names in one pattern are errors. Existing local names and global constants retain the pre-existing value-pattern meaning; they are not silently shadowed by bindings. Patterns must have the same tuple shape as their scrutinee. An entirely irrefutable tuple pattern, e.g. (a, _), counts as a catch-all. The exhaustiveness checker does not attempt product-space proofs: (true, _) and (false, _) alone still need an irrefutable fallback. Recursive enum payload patterns fn read(event: Event) -> i64 { return match event { Event::Nested(Inner::Number(0)) => 100, Event::Nested(Inner::Number(n)) if n > 10 => n, Event::Nested(_) => -1, Event::Data((1..10, true)) => 1, Event::Data((number, _)) => number, Event::Stop => 0, } } Nested patterns are type-checked against the corresponding payload field. The lowered control flow verifies each constructor tag before loading any of that constructor's payloads. Failed inner matches continue to the next outer arm; bindings become available only after the whole pattern matches.
<veriayrimi>
The parser accepts integer ranges inside tuple fields and constructor arguments, not only at the top level. Ranges remain compile-time-bounded integer patterns; 1..10 excludes 10, whereas 1..=10 includes it. These are pattern-context rules, not new general-purpose range values. Coverage is deliberately conservative: a constructor contributes full variant coverage only when every payload pattern is irrefutable. Some(7) does not cover every Some; Some(n) and Some(_) do. Even a set of specific patterns that a human can prove exhaustive may still require a fallback. Guarded arms never contribute coverage. Full recursive ADT coverage proofs are not claimed. These extensions apply to normal runtime matching. The limited compile-time interpreter is not expanded into a full ADT evaluator by this change. Guarded wildcards fn sign(value: i64) -> i64 { return match value { _ if value < 0 => -1, _ if value == 0 => 0, _ => 1, } } _ if condition is now a normal, ordered, guarded arm. Its condition must be bool; guarded patterns never establish exhaustiveness. An unguarded _ or else must be last, and duplicate default arms are rejected instead of silently replacing an earlier arm. Use _ if, not else if, in match syntax. Stronger type and structural checks Match guards must be boolean. Patterns must be compatible with their scrutinee type. Matching an integer against a string or an enum variant from another type is rejected. Value-producing arms must be compatible with the first non-diverging arm's result type. A branch that definitely returns or otherwise diverges does not constrain the result type. Mixing a void arm and a value arm is an error. This is deliberately not a new, general-purpose least-upper-bound inference algorithm. Enum payload patterns must have the declared arity and compatible field types. They support literals, bindings, wildcards, tuples, nested enum constructors, and constant integer ranges. Payload matching and binding are recursive. Bindings in p1 | p2 alternatives are rejected until common binding-set and path-sensitive lowering support is implemented. Binding-free alternatives retain their existing behavior. Range patterns require compatible integer bounds that are compile-time constants. Unsigned ranges now use unsigned IR comparisons, including values above MAX_I64. Tuple destructuring declarations require a tuple with exactly the same arity. Inferred tuples preserve integer widths/signedness and all type metadata representable by the existing type-spec machinery instead of defaulting most fields to i64. Tuples exceeding the existing eight-field descriptor capacity fail explicitly. Nested tuple compatibility is checked recursively. These changes intentionally reject some programs previously accepted with unsafe or misleading behavior. They do not add named fields, rest patterns, array/slice patterns or full recursive ADT coverage analysis. Regression gates zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseFast python3 tests/core_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir python3 tests/backend_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir python3 tests/cli_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir zig build test The core suite runs three executable fixtures through native ELF, IR VM, host C11, and wasm32-wasi at O0/O1/O2 (36 executions), and checks 31 invalid programs for both failure and the relevant diagnostic. It requires a C compiler and Node 22+ or Wasmtime, like the backend suite. It runs in CI alongside existing tests. Examples: examples/tuple-patterns.lir: nested tuple matching, literals, bindings, boolean/string fields, guarded fallthrough, guarded wildcards. examples/typed-match.lir: explicit generic identity, Option payload bindings, u8-preserving tuple inference, high unsigned ranges, and returning arms mixed with value arms. examples/enum-patterns.lir: literal/range payloads, nested constructors, tuples inside payloads, string payload matching, guard fallthrough, and safe skipping of fields belonging to unmatched variants. Next core-language work Normalize pattern representation instead of reusing expression AST shapes. Extend the conservative payload coverage model to prove unions of payload subspaces (e.g. A(true) plus A(false)), with independently checked tests. Implement binding-set unification for alternative patterns. Improve generic inference from typed locals, nested generic calls, and contextual Option/Result constructors. Nested calls such as identity(Some(11)) are not established as supported by this phase; explicit generic arguments and typed constructor-returning helpers remain useful. Unify tuple, closure, function, and branch type joins before claiming complete inference, then add lifetime/borrow analysis as a separately tested subsystem. LIR is a native systems-language compiler, not a C# compiler or a .NET runtime. It cannot currently run C# source, load CLR assemblies, or use the .NET Base Class Library. This phase adds a bounded language feature; it does not establish C# compatibility or parity with every C# operation. Implemented: named arguments and optional parameters fn main() -> i64 { printInt(calculate(1)) // 183 printInt(calculate(c: 9, a: 2)) // 289 printInt(calculate(2, c: 5)) // 285 return 0 } Contract Supported on statically resolved, direct free-function calls. Function pointers/closures, methods, trait dispatch, enum constructors, and builtins do not accept named arguments in this phase; unsupported forms are diagnosed. Parameter names are case-sensitive declaration names. Unknown names, duplicates, positional/named collisions, missing required parameters, and excess arguments are errors. Types are checked against the named destination, not the argument's source position. Positional arguments must precede all named arguments. This is an intentionally smaller rule than all combinations accepted by modern C#. Supplied argument expressions are evaluated exactly once, left to right in source order, then their values are placed in parameter order. Reordering labels must not reorder side effects. Parameters with defaults must be trailing. Defaults are type-checked at the declaration, even if no caller omits them. Defaults accept scalar compile-time constant expressions or string literals. Integer, boolean, and f64 defaults are exercised by the execution tests. They cannot invoke runtime functions, allocate containers, or depend on parameter values. Default expressions are resolved in declaration/global context, not against caller locals. Imported defaults retain their source context. Defaults are substituted at direct call sites. The function ABI and parameter count are unchanged. Function values still require the full positional signature; defaults are not attached to an indirect function value. Methods and entry-point main parameters cannot declare defaults in this phase. The existing limit of eight parameters/arguments still applies. Simple generic inference now associates supplied named arguments with the right declaration parameter before inference. This does not solve general nested or constraint-driven generic inference. Named/defaulted calls are explicitly rejected when the limited scalar compile-time evaluator attempts to execute them. This prevents early folding from bypassing semantic validation. A const value can still be used as a default expression; invoking an optional-parameter function in const or comptime evaluation is a separate, unsupported capability. The test suite checks two fixtures across native ELF, IR VM, host C11 and wasm32-wasi at O0/O1/O2 (24 executions), plus 20 invalid programs. Fixtures cover source-order side effects, omitted parameters, mixed positional/named calls, string/bool/float defaults, simple generic name-based inference, and imported defaults that must not capture caller variables. A C toolchain and Node 22+ or Wasmtime are required, as for the existing backend matrix. CI runs this gate. Capabilities still needed for a C#-scale environment Some existing LIR facilities (traits, closures, generics, Option/Result, threads, pattern matching) address related use cases, but they are not drop-in substitutes for the corresponding C# or .NET semantics. Runtime and object-model decisions must precede a credible promise of ecosystem-wide parity. Belgenin statüsü: Bu, HYPERION mimari tanımının iddialarının bağımsız değerlendirmesidir. Bu repodaki kurala uyar: hiçbir iddia, kanıtlanmış veya ölçülmüş olandan fazlasını söyleyemez. Dolayısıyla aşağıdaki her satır ya "gerçek/ürün", ya "kısmen gerçek (sınırlı alan)", ya "araştırma prototipi", ya da "teorem/fizik ile çelişiyor" olarak etiketlenir — ve gerekçesi verilir. İşaretler: ✅ bugün üründe · 🟡 kısmen gerçek, sınırlı alanda · 🔶 araştırma prototipi · ❌ matematik/fizik ile çelişiyor · ⚠️ sayısal iddia birkaç büyüklük mertebesi hatalı HYPERION, gerçek ve önemli araştırma yönlerini (bellek-içi hesaplama, veri akışı yürütme, çok sürümlü kod, erasure-coded hesaplama, CXL havuzlama) tek bir anlatıda birleştiriyor. Bileşenlerin çoğu gerçek; ama iki iddia kanıtlanmış teoremlerle, bir iddia da ışık hızıyla çelişiyor; ve neredeyse tüm sayısal süreler 1–4 büyüklük mertebesi hatalı. Neden bazı iddialar sadece "iyimser" değil, imkânsız 1.1 ORAM'de sıfır gecikme yoktur (iddia 7) Goldreich–Ostrovsky (1996) her ORAM için Ω(log n) bant genişliği alt sınırı verdi; Larsen–Nielsen (CRYPTO 2018) bunu online ORAM'lar için, yalnızca hesaplama güvenliği varsayarak ve verinin keyfî biçimde kodlanmasına izin vererek kanıtladı. OptORAMa (EUROCRYPT 2020) bu sınıra ulaşan ilk şemadır — yani log n maliyeti aşılamaz, sadece "optimal" hale getirilebilir.
<veriayrimi>
Sonuç: "sıfır gecikmeli ORAM" bir mühendislik eksiği değil, bir teorem ihlalidir. Dürüst versiyon: oblivious erişim, log n ek yükle mümkündür ve bu ek yük donanım hızlandırıcılarıyla sabit katsayıya indirilebilir. 1.2 Işık hızı bir bütçe kısıtıdır (iddia 11) Boşlukta ışık: 30 cm/ns. Fiberde (n ≈ 1.47): ~20 cm/ns. Tokyo–Frankfurt büyük daire ≈ 9.400 km; fiber güzergâhı tipik olarak 1.3–1.5× → tek yön ~60–70 ms, gözlenen RTT ~120–250 ms. 3 GHz'de bu, ~2×10⁸ çevrim demektir. Bir L4 cache miss'i ~40 çevrimdir. 2×10⁸ çevrimlik bir "cache katmanı" cache değildir; coğrafi replikasyon + gevşek tutarlılık modelidir. CXL 3.0'ın bellek havuzlama/paylaşımı gerçek — ama aynı raf/oda ölçeğinde (yüz nanosaniye). Kıtalararası tek adres uzayı, tutarlılık protokollerinin değil, fiziğin sınırıdır; bu yüzden gerçek sistemler (Spanner, CRDT'ler) mantıksal saat ve gevşek tutarlılık kullanır. 1.3 Sayısal bütçe tutarsız (iddia 12) 2.5 ns'lik uçtan uca "yaşam döngüsü", tek bir DRAM erişiminden 40× kısa. Ayrıca 0.2 ns < 1 çevrim; "Shannon entropisini ölçüp sıkıştırıp CXL'e yazıp lens oluşturmak" tek bir çevrimden küçük bir bütçeye sığmaz — entropi ölçümü akışın tamamını görmeyi gerektirir. 1.4 "Sıfır cezalı" spekülasyon mümkün değil (iddia 6) Spekülasyonun üç ayrı maliyeti var ve hiçbiri sıfır değil: Yanlış tahmin → iş boşa gider (enerji + ALU zamanı). Yan etki disiplini → yalnızca saf hesaplar speküle edilebilir; yan etkili kodu (yazma, IO, serbest bırakma) speküle etmek geri alınamaz. Yan kanal → "paylaşılan cache durumunu asla değiştirme" kuralı, tam da sabit-zamanlı (constant-time) hesaplama demektir; bu da tipik olarak belirgin bir yavaşlama getirir. Yani Spectre/Meltdown'a bağışık ve bedava spekülasyon, aynı anda olamaz. Gerçek karşılığı: iyimser paralelleştirme (TLS), değer tahmini ve zararsız (rollback edilebilir) saf gelecekler — hepsi ölçülmüş kazanç/zarar oranıyla değerlendirilir. Bunlar HYPERION'un değil, zaten var olan mühendisliğin parçaları — ve tam da bu yüzden ilginç: PIM / DPU / computational storage (iddia 4). UPMEM PIM DIMM'ler, Samsung HBM-PIM (Aquabolt-XL), SK hynix AiM, NVIDIA BlueField, SmartSSD — hepsi üründe. Sınır: bu birimlere her kod gitmez; ancak dar, veri-paralel çekirdekler (map/filter/reduce, lineer cebir) offload edilir. "Kanıt-taşıyan kapsül" fikrinin gerçek karşılığı, sınırlı bir DSL için derleyicinin doğrulanmış çekirdek üretmesidir. Bariyersiz çalıştırma (iddia 5). Global epoch yok; happens-before var. Rayon/TBB/OpenMP task'ları bunu yıllardır yapıyor. PDTF'ye göre gerçek bir mimari fark — ama yeni fizik değil, iyi bilinen veri akışı modeli. Çok sürümlü kod + çalışma anında seçim (iddia 10). CPU dispatch (ifunc), PGO, otomatik vektörleştiricinin seçtiği varyantlar, polymorphic inline cache'ler: hepsi "önceden derlenmiş, kanıtlanmış varyantlar arasında atlama"dır. 3 çevrimde kod seçmek inandırıcı (bir dallanma); 3 çevrimde veri yapısını değiştirmek değil. Sıkıştırılmış bellek yolu (iddia 3). IBM z15/z16 bellek sıkıştırması, GPU'larda kayıpsız sıkıştırma gerçek. Sınır: veri sıkıştırılabilir olmalı ve sıkıştırma gecikme + alan maliyeti getirir. Coded computing (iddia 9). Lee ve ark. (2017) polinom kodlarıyla dağıtık lineer cebirde straggler/kayıp toleransı gösterdi; depolamada Reed-Solomon standart. Sınır: cebirsel iş yükleri; genel amaçlı "sıfır rollback" yok (tek kopyası olan hareketli durum ölürse kurtarma zaman alır — PACELC). Kuantum-dirençli kriptografi (iddia 8'in gerçek yarısı). NIST FIPS 203 (ML-KEM), 204 (ML-DSA), 205 (SLH-DSA) 2024'te standardlaştı. Kafes tabanlı anahtar işlemleri ucuz; kafes tabanlı hesaplama (FHE) değil. Bu bölüm, FY'daki en abartılı ve en az kanıtlı kısım — ve hakkında en çok veri olan kısım. Gerçek olan: DeepMind'ın AlphaDev'i (Nature, 2023) sıralama ağlarını RL ile gerçekten keşfetti ve LLVM libc++'a girdi. Ama ölçek şu: Yani "literatürde adı olmayan hibrit Radix-SIMD-Bitonic ağ algoritması sentezleme" iddiasının gerçek karşılığı: dar, ölçülebilir, doğrulanabilir bir alt alanda birkaç komutluk iyileştirme. Bu bile büyük bir başarı. Gerçek olmayan: "milyarlarca algoritma uzayını tarayıp genel amaçlı yeni bir sıralama algoritması sentezlemek". Sıralamanın asimptotik alt sınırı Ω(n log n)'dir ve bu bir teoremdir; sentez bunu değiştiremez, yalnızca sabit katsayıyı ve sabit boyutlu ağları iyileştirebilir. Ayrıca arama uzayı üstel büyür: AlphaDev'in kendisi sort > 8 için "hesaplama açısından yasak" diyor. HQTT eleştirisi: Homotopi Tip Teorisi (univalence) ile Niceliksel Tip Teorisi (Atkey/McBride; Idris 2'deki lineerlik) ayrı ayrı gerçek ve aktiftir. Ama: Univalence, genel haliyle karar verilemez ve kanoniklik/computation'ı bozar; cubical tip teorisi bunu kısmen onarır, bedeli ağır. Kaynak muhasebesi için gerçek araçlar QTT değil, AARA (otomatik amortize kaynak analizi) ve Granule gibi lineer/grade'li sistemlerdir. "Tip başına pJ" bugün türetilemez: enerji, tipin değil komut dizisinin ve veri hareketinin fonksiyonudur. Ölçülebilir bir yaklaşım var: derleyicinin IR'i üzerinden çevrim/bayt modeli (bu repo tam olarak bunu yapabilir — bkz. §5). Bunlar zevk meselesi değil, tasarım tutarsızlıkları: "Veri hiç hareket etmez" vs "PIM'e kapsül fırlatılır". Eğer hesaplama veriye gidiyorsa, sonuç geri gelmek zorunda; "sıfır hareket" yalnızca ara hareketler için geçerli. Ayrıca transpoze bir görünüm üzerinden hesaplama, donanımda strided erişim üretir: kopyalamayı erteleyip cache ıskalamasına dönüştürürsünüz. "Küresel zaman yok" vs "her 100 µs'de telemetri okuyup 3 çevrimde karar ver". Telemetri döngüsü tam da küresel bir senkronizasyondur; PMU okuması ucuz değildir ve çekirdekler arası karar için bir noktada anlaşma gerekir. "Sıfır bayt kopya" vs "Shannon sınırında sıkıştırma". Sıkıştırılmış gösterim, farklı bir kodlamadır; ALU'ya girerken açmak zorunludur, bu da genişletme (unpack) işidir — yani bir tür hareket. "Kanıt-taşıyan kapsül" vs "her kodu gönderebilme". Doğrulanmış çekirdek göndermek, çekirdek dilinin toplam olmasını (total, yan etkisiz, sınırlı) gerektirir; o zaman genel amaçlı dil iddiası düşer. Bu reponun ölçütü net: derlenen, çalışan ve test edilen şey iddia edilir. Bu ölçütle HYPERION'un altı fikri aşamalı olarak somutlaştırılabilir. Aşağıdaki sıralama "etki / gerçekleştirilebilirlik" oranına göredir ve her adımın ölçümü var. 5.1 Çok-sürümlü kod + çalışma anında seçim (en yüksek oran) LIR zaten IR üretiyor ve -O0/-O1/-O2 varyantlarını derleyebiliyor. Yapılabilir olan: aynı fonksiyonun ölçülmüş olarak farklı veri yoğunluklarında farklı varyantlarla derlenmesi ve çalışma anında seçilmesi. Ölçüt: bir benchmark'ta (seyrek vs yoğun matris) tek-varyantlı derlemeye göre ölçülebilir kazanç; bench/ içinde tekrarlanabilir. Dürüst sınır: veri yapısı değişmez; yalnızca kod yolu değişir. 5.2 Sıfır-kopya görünümler (view) — doğru tanımla []T zaten var; eksik olan yeniden yorumlama (reinterpret) primitifi ve hizalama/aliasing kuralları. Gerçek kazanç: transpoze/filtre görünümü O(1) kurmak; sonrasında erişimin strided olduğunu ve cache maliyeti taşıdığını belgelemek. Ölçüt: 1 GB'lık matriste transpoze görünüm kurma < 100 ns; görünüm üzerinden toplama, kopyalı sürüme göre ölçülen bir yavaşlama ile raporlanır. Bu, "0 bayt kopya" iddiasının kanıtlanabilir halidir. 5.3 Sadece saf fonksiyonlar için spekülasyon LIR'de #[pure] zaten var ve sema onu denetliyor. Bu, spekülasyonun güvenli olduğu tek alan: yan etkisiz bir çağrıyı iyimser hesaplayıp sonucu atabilirsiniz. Ölçüt: gecikmeli bir bağımlılıkta (@chan_recv beklerken) saf çağrıyı önden hesaplamanın kazancı; yanlış tahminde kayıp raporlanır. Dürüst sınır: yan kanal izolasyonu ek maliyet getirir; bu ölçülmeden "sıfır ceza" denemez. 5.4 Tipte kaynak bütçesi — HoTT değil, ölçülmüş model [cost(cycles<=N, bytes<=M)] gibi bir öznitelik; düz çizgili (branch-free) tam sayı fonksiyonlarda IR üzerinden çevrim modeliyle doğrulanır. LIR'in VM'i sayesinde modeli ölçümle kalibre etmek mümkün. Ölçüt: modelin tahmini ile gerçek çevrim sayısı arasındaki sapma (ör. ±%10) ve modelin reddettiği/geçirdiği örneklerin test edilmesi. 5.5 Bariyersiz görev grafiği spawn/@chan_ var; eksik olan bağımlılık grafiğinden otomatik zamanlama. Bir kütüphane düzeyinde DAG zamanlayıcı gerçekçi ve küresel bariyer gerektirmez. 5.6 Offload çekirdekleri (PIM/DPU) — araştırma, ama en gerçekçi uzun vade Dar bir "çekirdek DSL'i" (map/filter/reduce) tanımlayıp, hedefe (CPU / SIMD / PIM) derleyen bir arka uç. Bugün yapılabilecek kısmı: aynı çekirdeğin CPU ve SIMD varyantlarını üretip ölçmek. 5.7 Yapılmaması gerekenler ORAM'i "sıfır gecikme" olarak sunmak (teorem ihlali). Kıtalararası tek adres uzayı vaadi (fizik ihlali). "Her tip şu kadar pJ" (ölçülemez; yalnızca dizi/komut düzeyinde modellenir). Genel amaçlı "algoritmayı sistem icat eder" (Ω(n log n) ve arama uzayı).
<veriayrimi>
HYPERION'un değerli çekirdeği şu üç cümleye indirgenebilir ve üçü de savunulabilir: Hesaplamayı veriye gönder (PIM/DPU/computational storage) — gerçek, üründe, dar çekirdeklerle sınırlı. Küresel bariyer yerine bağımlılık tabanlı zamanlama — gerçek, standart, doğru yön. Tek bir ikili yerine, önceden doğrulanmış varyantlar arasında çalışma anında seçim — gerçek, ölçülebilir, bugün yapılabilir. Geri kalanı (sıfır gecikmeli ORAM, kıtalararası tek adres uzayı, 2.5 ns'lik uçtan uca döngü, sıfır cezalı yan-kanalsız spekülasyon, tip başına pJ muhasebesi, genel amaçlı algoritma sentezi) ya kanıtlanmış teoremlerle ya ışık hızıyla ya da termodinamikle çelişiyor. Bu, fikirlerin değersiz olduğu anlamına gelmez: her iddianın ölçülebilir bir versiyonu var ve doğru sıra, en ucuz ölçümden başlamaktır. Bu reponun kuralı tam olarak bunu söylüyor: önce ölç, sonra iddia et.* Kaynaklar Larsen & Nielsen, "Yes, There is an Oblivious RAM Lower Bound!", CRYPTO 2018 — https://eprint.iacr.org/2018/423 Asharov ve ark., "OptORAMa: Optimal Oblivious RAM", EUROCRYPT 2020 — https://eprint.iacr.org/2018/892 Mankowitz ve ark., "Faster sorting algorithms discovered using deep reinforcement learning", Nature 2023 — https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-023-06004-9 Lee ve ark., "Speeding Up Distributed Machine Learning Using Codes", 2017 (coded computing) NIST FIPS 203/204/205 (ML-KEM, ML-DSA, SLH-DSA), 2024 CXL 3.0/3.1 spec (bellek havuzlama ve paylaşım) Bu belgedeki ayrımlar (✅ üründe / 🟡 kısmen / 🔶 prototip / ❌ çelişiyor) lir cmf tarafından denetlenen alan yüzeyine dönüştürüldü: Ölçüm kuralı: her doğrulanmış varyant IR VM'de çalıştırılır ve raporlanan tek maliyet gerçekten yürütülen komut sayısıdır. "3 çevrim", "2.5 ns", "pJ/tip" gibi hiçbir sayı bu repoda üretilmez — çünkü ölçülmemiştir. These features are implemented in the Zig bootstrap frontend and lowered to portable IR. The execution matrix checks Linux x86-64 native ELF, IR VM, host C11, and wasm32-wasi at -O0, -O1, and -O2. This does not establish support for all target architectures or constitute a complete language-safety guarantee. Unconditional loops loop is now a reserved keyword. It is a statement lowered to while true and uses the existing loop-scope semantics for break, continue, return, and defer. Nested loops are supported. Loop values, labeled exits, and break value are not added by this change. Existing spin-loop diagnostics still apply. Unary plus +expression is numeric identity, supported for integer and floating-point operands, including constant expressions. It does not convert a string or bool to a number. It evaluates its operand once. Unary signs bind less tightly than exponentiation and more tightly than multiplication. printInt(2 3 2) // 512: right-associative printInt(-2 2) // -4: exponentiation precedes unary minus printInt((-2) 3) // -8 printInt(2 * 3 2) // 18 const limit = 3 4 // 81: constant evaluation is supported Both operands must be i64. Floating-point power and implicit width/signedness conversions are not supported. Operands are evaluated once, base before exponent. The exponent must be nonnegative: a statically known negative exponent is a compile-time error; a dynamic negative exponent traps at runtime. Trap exit codes and messages remain backend-specific, but execution must not succeed. The result is computed modulo 2^64 and interpreted as signed i64, consistently at compile time and runtime. 0 0 is defined as 1. Exponentiation by squaring requires at most 63 iterations for a nonnegative i64 exponent; it does not loop once per exponent value. The compound assignment = is also available on mutable i64 variables. It uses the same wrapping result and exponent rules; the target is evaluated once. A statically known negative exponent is rejected, while a dynamic negative exponent traps at runtime. All arguments must be i64. Except for the boolean predicate, results are i64. Use explicit casts when passing other integer widths or signedness. Bit-pattern operations interpret the input as 64 bits, not as an arbitrarily wide integer. Rotation by 0 or a multiple of 64 is identity; negative counts normalize in the same way (@rotr(1, -1) is 2). Neither rotation generates an illegal shift by @bit_reverse(1) is MIN_I64. @is_power_of_two(0) and @is_power_of_two(MIN_I64) are false. Arguments are evaluated exactly once, left-to-right, even when lowering uses a value multiple times. These intrinsics are runtime expressions that can be optimized through normal IR passes. Support for evaluating arbitrary intrinsic calls in const or comptime contexts is not introduced by this change; the power operator and unary plus have explicit constant-evaluator integration. Explicit integer-to-float conversions Integer-to-float casts preserve the source integer's signedness across all execution backends. Signed values use signed conversion; unsigned values (in particular u64 values with bit 63 set) use unsigned conversion and therefore remain nonnegative. Casting to f32 rounds to IEEE-754 binary32 precision before widening the compiler's canonical float slot back to binary64 storage. Float-to-integer casts truncate toward zero. Unsigned destinations use an unsigned conversion path, accept values in [0, 2^64), and trap on negative, NaN, or out-of-range inputs. Signed destinations use the signed i64 range and trap on NaN or out-of-range inputs. Narrow integer destinations then retain the low target-width bits (sign-extending signed results and zero-extending unsigned results), matching the language's explicit integer narrowing behavior. fn main() -> i64 { let high = ((9223372036854775807 as u64 + 1 as u64) as f64) let max = ((-1) as u64) as f64 let rounded = ((16777217 as u32) as f32) as f64 // high == 2^63; max rounds to 2^64 in binary64; // rounded == 16777216 because binary32 cannot represent 16777217. if high == 9223372036854775808.0 and max == 18446744073709551616.0 and rounded == 16777216.0 { return 0 } return 1 } The test matrix checks zero, small unsigned values, the signed boundary, u32 maximum, u64 maximum, binary32 rounding, unsigned float-to-integer values above i64::MAX, the u64 boundary, and narrowing casts through native ELF, IR VM, C11, and wasm32-wasi at -O0, -O1, and -O2. Negative, NaN, and out-of-range unsigned conversions trap. This does not add 128-bit integers. Regression coverage zig build python3 tests/backend_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir python3 tests/cli_test.py ./zig-out/bin/lir zig build test The backend suite requires a host C compiler and Node 22+ or Wasmtime. It now runs twelve positive fixtures at three optimization levels on four backends (144 executions), plus 24 dynamic-negative-exponent trap checks for and =. Numeric expected values include independent Python integer/bit-pattern calculations, not merely agreement between LIR backends. Diagnostics cover invalid operand types, argument counts, negative constant exponents, and malformed loop syntax. Generated test programs and compiler outputs live in temporary directories. Runnable examples: examples/loop-control.lir examples/integer-power.lir examples/integer-intrinsics.lir The float identity example exposed a pre-existing native printFloat bug: the printer freed a pointer eight bytes into its allocation. The native printer now passes the allocation base to free, preserving heap-header validation rather than disabling its trap. Generated by lir lang --markdown. Do not edit by hand: change src/bootstrap/langref.zig and regenerate. Every supported example below is compiled and executed by zig build test. Status: supported = safe to generate, partial = read the notes, legacy = accepted but prefer the canonical spelling, unsupported = rejected. Reserved words Never emit these as identifiers, parameter names, field names or function names. The list comes from the lexer (lir lang --keywords --json, schema lir.keywords.v1) and includes words that are reserved but not implemented (async, await, unsafe, asm, extern, mod, i128, …). fn, let, var, mut, const, struct, enum, trait, impl, loop, match, if, else, while, for, unless, until, guard, proc, in, return, break, continue, defer, pub, mod, use, import, type, extern, self, true, false, null, comptime, try, panic, elif, and, or, i8, i16, i32, i64, u8, u16, u32, u64, f32, f64, bool, string, void, unsafe, volatile, asm, align, packed, union, unreachable, as, isize, usize, i128, u128, never, dyn, spawn, async, await, where Declarations fn — supported Top-level only: no nested/named-inner functions. Every non-void function must return a value on all paths. Statements are newline separated; ';' is optional and rarely needed. Maximum 8 parameters. Global constants only; there are no global mutable variables. The type annotation is optional. Constant expressions may use arithmetic, '' and comptime calls. Fields are newline separated, not comma separated. Field declarations may not name another struct type (use a pointer to it). Layout is 8-byte slots with documented offsets (lir ast shows them). Maximum 32 fields. Variants are comma separated. A variant may carry up to 8 payload fields. Construct with Name::Variant(...), compare with ==. Matching an enum requires exhaustive arms. fn main() -> i64 { let s = Shape::Circle(7) return match s { Shape::Circle(r) => r 2, Shape::Empty => 0, } } union — supported Untagged C-style union of 8-byte slots. Reading a field other than the one written is not type-checked. Structs + impl blocks: the closest thing LIR has to methods, but there is no inheritance, no embedding and no operator overloading. Methods receive 'self' (read-only) or 'self: mut Self' (mutable). Call with value syntax p.m(); the receiver is auto-referenced. There is no 'impl Trait for Type' free-standing method block other than trait impls.
